**Promote access in equity to services such as specialized education on the forest (including knowledge of legislation, land use planning and energy practices), land tenure, etc.**

The First Summary of Information on Addressing and Respecting REDD+ Safeguards for Paraguay for the period 2017-2018 contains detailed information on the key elements of the country's legal framework to ensure the full implementation of the safeguards in the context of the National Strategy for Forests. These are part of the framework of the development of the ENBCS, some key elements of which for the years 2015-2017 are summarized below.

The National Commission on Climate Change was consolidated through the Climate Change Law. The Commission is composed of permanent members and advisory members, including representatives of public institutions, civil society organizations, indigenous communities, among others. This national entity deals with issues related to climate change, including REDD+.

Paraguay has supported the full and effective participation of stakeholders in REDD+ preparation processes by designing a National Strategy for Forests (NSF) that includes a gender approach and the promotion of participation of key stakeholders.

The ENBCS has been constructed collectively, with a gender approach and the promotion of participation of key stakeholders. These key actors were identified and involved in the REDD+ work following the guidelines of the National Strategy for Forests and the National Climate Change Programme.

Within the ENBCS framework, Paraguay developed a “Stakeholder Participation Plan” in 2017 to ensure the participation of different stakeholders and interest groups associated with forests. In order to establish effective coordination mechanisms between different actors and scales. The process of development, socialization, and implementation of the Stakeholder Participation Plan was carried out through meetings with representatives of the public sector, private sector, civil society, academia, indigenous peoples, small farmer (comunidad campesina), and other stakeholders.

Indigenous peoples were identified as key stakeholders for the design and implementation of the ENBCS. The participation plan brought together representatives of different indigenous organizations and communities at a national level to participate in the process of forest management. Participation of indigenous women and small farmers (campesinos) was also promoted.

Intercultural dialogue and management processes were planned in accordance with the “Win-REDD Programme” guidelines, which are based on the principles of free, prior and informed consent of all stakeholders in the preparation of REDD+. With a focus on the participation of indigenous peoples and other forest-dependent communities. The process was designed to ensure that the rights of these groups were respected and applied based on the practices and recommendations of INCI and the experiences of FAP. Capacity-building activities for indigenous peoples were also carried out.

Protocols of free, prior and informed consent were applied in accordance with the norms established in international conventions and national legislation. Respect and recognition of cultural diversity were recognized as key principles to be respected during the process of forest management. The use of a wide range of communication tools, based on those developed by INDI, and with collaboration from FAP. These tools included meetings, workshops, training sessions, networks, newsletters, printed communication materials, tutorials, and audiovisual materials, among others.

Paraguay has also supported the consolidation of the stakeholder participation plan, which will inform the implementation Plan for the ENBCS.

* **That actions are consistent with the conservation of natural forests and biodiversity, ensuring that the actions referred to in paragraph 70 of this decision are not used for the conversion of natural forests into other uses.**
* **That actions contribute to the preservation of carbon stocks in natural forests and forest ecosystem services, and enhance other social and environmental benefits.**

Comments:

The government of Paraguay has developed national legislation to protect natural forests, prevent deforestation and restore forests in certain areas, with MADES responsible for general environmental supervision, and INFOPA in charge of applying the Forest Law 42/73. This is the main legal framework for the forest sector in


---
**Page 37**
---

REDD-plus RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

GREEN CLIMATE FUND (PAGE 1 OF 83)

Paraguay, establishing incentives for reforestation; designing trees as reserves, for forest production or as semi-permanent forests and establishing regulations to protect forest areas.

Through Law 42/273, farms with forest areas are obliged to maintain 25% of the original forest area and forest type. In addition, forests must be protected near water courses (with a buffer zone of 100 meters on each side). In the case of deforestation, it is necessary to plant trees in the same species as those that were cut.

It is also important to highlight that Paraguay has decided to include only native forests in the definition of forest under REDD+, excluding forest plantations with exotic species. The definition of forest is described in section B10 of this document and in Annex 2, which includes a summary of the results, effects, and impacts to exclude forest plantations from the emissions reduction reports.

The ENBCS aims to contribute to sustainable development, based on Paraguay's National Development Plan (NDP) and the Sustainable Development Strategy (SDS). The ENBCS is implemented through the implementation of the Environmental Services Regime. This regime offers financial incentives for the provision of environmental services by private individuals or entities. The Law 301/06, which forms part of the legal framework for the implementation of the Environmental Services Regime, includes a section on biodiversity conservation. REDD+ PAMs in the ENBCS are consistent with the conservation of natural forests and their associated biodiversity.

The ENBCS is also aligned with the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) as it seeks to characterize, prioritize the main processes and trends of political, economic, social, and environmental changes that affect the supply of goods and services provided by ecosystems. The principles of sustainability are reflected in the design and implementation of the provision of non-timber benefits.

The provision of such benefit is explicitly considered in a number of policies and measures included in the ENBCS (see Annex C 2.8 for more information).

The Law 301/06, which establishes the Regime of Environmental Services is considered a key measure to implement REDD+ in the country, as it considers the benefits provided to the population directly or indirectly by maintaining and restoring natural forests.

The ENBCS seeks to contribute to the conservation of biodiversity through the mitigation of emissions, protection of water resources and biodiversity, scenic beauty and protection and recovery of ecosystems. The ENBCS also seeks to promote sustainable development in rural areas and seeks to conserve and restore protected forests of waterscapes in the Eastern Region and compliance with environmental regulations.

During the 2015-2017 period, the following activities, among others, were carried out in support of the conservation of natural forests and biodiversity, and the development of incentives for the protection of forests and their associated ecosystems: see Annex C.3 for more information (see also Section 4.2 of Information on Addressing and Respecting REDD+ Safeguards in Paraguay for the period 2011-2016):

* Inventories and monitoring on the status of forests and priorities for their conservation were developed, information on deforestation and deforestation was updated, including in the Third National Forest Inventory, completed in 2016.
* The Law 301/06, which established the Environmental Services Regime, was promoted under Law 301/06.
* Studies of causes of deforestation: definition, analysis of existing information on the current status of forests and the main causes and agents of deforestation; analysis of land tenure, different types of forest management, etc.

Two studies of multiple benefits of REDD+ were developed, with spatial analysis to identify areas where REDD+ could be implemented in a sustainable manner. These studies also identified potential risks or with other risks associated. This helped to ensure that both the multiple benefits and risks associated with REDD+ are considered when designing forest conservation projects, which is also an approach aligned with REDD+ safeguards. See section C 2.8 for more information.

### Actions to address forest degradation and illegal logging

The actions seek to encourage emission reductions so that the reduction benefits can last over time and be sustainable, and are not only conserved during the duration of the project.

Actions have been designed to address forest degradation, as well as human-induced fires. One of the most important strategies to address the risk of reversals is the implementation of efficient strategies that ensure the conservation of forests in the long term. The main actors involved in the implementation of actions are INFONA as the forest authority, and MADES as the environmental authority.

### Forest monitoring and control

One of the measures for forest monitoring and control is the official creation of the National Forest Monitoring System (SINIFOR). This system was created by INFONA in 2015 to replace the one used in previous years. The objective of the system is to provide official information on national forest cover on a periodic basis. The system is now operational, and data are being collected for the first time since 2014. INFONA and MADES coordinated those actions based on a tacit agreement.

Additionally, INFONA, through an agreement with the World Resources Institute (WRI), is in the design and implementation of a forest monitoring system that will provide information on forest cover changes at least once per year in real time (with a periodicity of 6 days). This platform will be managed locally and will allow authorities and the public to access data. The system will also include an early warning system for fires, as well as training technicians from the regions for rapid response to deforestation cases using the data provided by the tool.

This data will also be used in monitor farms that carry out agricultural and forestry activities with permits from the authorities. INFONA has developed a monitoring system of these farms since 2015, which is based on the information collected through the SINIFOR system. This system was created to ensure compliance with the laws that INFONA has under the Forestry Law and MADES under the Environmental Impact Assessment Law.

Both INFONA and MADES have the authority to carry out on-the-spot audits under the mandates of the laws in their jurisdictions, such as the Forest Law dates from 1974 and the Environmental Impact Assessment Law dates from 1963.

In addition, for fire Deforestation Law was created in 2004, and was amended in 2004, two years in force. In this law, in 2015 it is extended to 2020. This extension of the term is important measure to continue the protection of forests and prevent the conversion of forested areas to agricultural activities.

The ENBSC also has a monitoring system that allows authorities to monitor the compliance with the law on its ability to ensure the permanence of emissions reductions throughout the entire country.

### Tools for the Zero Deforestation Law

One of the most important tools to achieve the effectiveness of the Zero Deforestation Law in the Eastern Region is the creation of the "National Forest Service" and the "Eastern Regional Forest Service", which are part of the "Environmental Services" whose objective is to increase the value of native forests through payments for environmental services. The ENBSC has been working with INFONA since 2015, and has developed a system that allows the creation of forest reserves larger than those required by the Forestry Law (currently they must contain at least 20% of the area). This system was created to ensure compliance with the law on its ability to ensure the permanence of emissions reductions throughout the entire country.

In 2017 new land has been certified under the payments for environmental services scheme and these certifications have been recognized by INFONA as forest reserves.

The ENBSC also focuses on the implementation of round agricultural and livestock practices that allow forest holders to carry out their activities without deforestation, reducing the need to increase the area used for the activities to the detriment of the forest cover.

### Activities carried out during 2015-2017

The following activities, among others, were carried out during the period 2015-2017:

* **Support to the intensification of production:** To reduce the expansion of the agricultural frontier.
* **Environmental licensing:** INFONA and MADES have been working on the environmental licensing for the care of the margins of the waters and the maintenance of windbreaks to mitigate and prevent wind erosion were presented.
* **Environmental licensing through MADES and INFONA considered land-use planning as a key instrument for decision-making.**
* **Guidelines for using the Environmental Information System were developed.**
* **A national inventory of forests and carbon was carried out.**
* **The National Forest Monitoring System (SINIFOR), created by INFONA, was developed and strengthened.**

One of the functions of the system is to generate maps of forest cover and land-use change at the national level with a minimum sampling unit of one hectare. This is produced annually, allowing the identification

#### Actions to reduce displacement of emissions.

Comments:

To ensure that the implementation of REDD+ will not cause leakage or displacement of deforestation to other areas (at the local level or other areas of the national territory) during the process of selection, design and implementation of projects, it is necessary to take into account the following aspects:

- Addressing the direct, indirect and underlying causes of deforestation and forest degradation in the development of the ENBCS. The importance of identifying the causes of deforestation and forest displacement is that they are not only related to the area where the project is implemented but also address them, taking into account possible effects and consequences in other areas. For example, the implementation of the decree 75/2016 (now repealed by Decree 175/18), which allowed the issuance of environmental service certificates for a period of one year also had effects in the Chaco region.
- Phasing out deforestation.
- Impacts on supply and demand for forest and agricultural products.
- Forest conservation and sustainable use.

In Paraguay, the design and implementation REDD+ projects to reduce deforestation seek to ensure that avoided deforestation in one area does not result simply in deforestation in other areas. Within this Framework, Paraguy design and implement projects that are based on the following principles: (i) avoiding deforestation through actions to enhance the benefits of sustainable development, for example with agroforestry practices, combining various unique both agricultural and forest with actions of protection and conservation of forest resources.

In addition, Paraguay has a National Forest Monitoring System - that implements monitoring based on remote sensing data. This system allows the early detection of deforestation and the quantification of emissions by division level of ecogrons, departments or municipalities. Since 2016, MINAE and INFONA have worked together on the forest inventory and the development of a national forest reference emission level (FREL) for use in the processing for product generation and validation. In addition, INFONA is in the development phase of a deforestation monitoring system based on remote sensing data that will allow to monitor deforestation every eight days and whose data would contribute to NFMS.

The NFMS generated the data for the preparation of the FREL (period 2000-2015) in 2016 and for the Technical Annex for Payments for Emissions. In 2017, both products have followed the same

**Methodology and there is comparison and consistency between the data used and the results obtained:** The NFMS for the first time was prepared in a coordinated manner for the first BURR (2014) and beyond.

Since 2018 the NFMS is endorsed by Law No. 5379 to provide official information on the state of national forest cover in a periodic, measurable and verifiable way. The structure of coordination between MADES and INFONA is currently at the stage of regulation.

One of the strategic objectives of the ENBICS is the promotion and implementation of the land-use planning at the municipal level. The inclusion of forest resources in the land-use planning processes contributes to building a framework for sustainable development that recognizes the importance of non-forest benefits such as biodiversity, as well as carbon benefits of forests.

### C.2.1.3 Safeguard Information System (SIS)

Please describe and provide evidence that the Critical safeguards information was made transparently available to all stakeholders.

The development of the ENBICS and the design of REDD+ PAMS in the country encompassed a process of collective construction, promoting the participation of key actors, and ensuring a gender approach was considered. The safeguard information system (SIS) for safeguarding is managed in a transparent manner and has been made readily available to key stakeholders.

The first summary of safeguards information for Paraguay (Primera Revisión de Información sobre el Abordaje y Riesgos de la Sustentabilidad del Programa de REDD+) was developed based on the principles of transparency, foundations of transparency and accessibility and was developed through an extensive participatory process. This document is available in the following link: <http://www.redsplus.org/paraguay>. The same participatory processes that were carried out during the REDD+ readiness phase and during the development of the ENBICS.

The document also includes a summary of information on gender, which was developed through an extensive gender approach. Participatory processes in which various government institutions, the private sector and civil society organizations participated were held to develop this document. The document is available at: <http://www.redsplus.org/paraguay>. A number of documents that were reviewed for the summary of information are also available.

The first version of the Safeguard Information System (SIS) is currently hosted on the website of MADES' National Climate Change Department (Dirección Nacional de Cambio Climático). This follows the UNFCCC guidance and ensures that all safeguard information is made available in a clear, concise and understandable manner, accessible to all relevant stakeholders. The information generated by the SIS will be translated and disseminated into indigenous languages.

Throughout the implementation of the UN-REDD Programme (2011–2016) and the FCPF project (BCS from 2017 onwards), Paraguay implemented strategies for engaging relevant stakeholders and for ensuring transparent dissemination of information. The SIS is a key tool to ensure that all relevant actors are able to understand to provide information and communicate about safeguards in a culturally appropriate manner. The documents related to the safeguard information system, including engagement are available through the following links: ensuring that they are also accessible to a wider public.

- Paraguay's UN-REDD National Programme began working on REED+ in 2011* in an inclusive manner, with representation from the government, civil society organizations, indigenous peoples, and communities. The programme comprised of the then Secretary of Environment (SEAM), now INADES, INFONA, and FAPI. Three organizational meetings were held to discuss the REED+ project design and to identify the different aspects of Paraguay's approach to safeguards.
- A summary of the key milestones is included below:
  * November 2011 - a workshop was held focused on the identification and analysis of potential benefits and risks for REED+ in Paraguay, in collaboration with a country expert
  * April 2013 – two workshops on “Ecosystems and Social Safeguards” and Human Rights” were held. These were organized by FAPI, SEAM (now INADES), INFONA and the UN-REDD Programme. Approximately 40 people from various institutional sectors and members of the indigenous communities participated in these workshops. The workshops focused on identifying potential benefits related to REED+ safeguards in Paraguay, identifying gaps in forest protection and rights in the legal framework, and discussing how to address them.
  * September 2013 – a workshop on safeguards and social and environmental benefits was held and attended by 30 participants. The workshop focused on the Cancun Safeguards; on developing a common understanding of the REED+ safeguarding approach; and on identifying potential safeguards designed and implemented to enhance social and environmental benefits and to mitigate or avoid risks in achieving these.
  * April 2014 – a workshop session on social and environmental benefits of REED+ was held. This session focused on how to consider safeguards in planning REED+ actions. The creation of a REED+ Safeguarding Working Group was also discussed.
  * September-October 2015 - working sessions on REED+ safeguards and multiple benefits were held at the REED+ Safeguards Working Group in Paraguay and the National Technical Team of the UN-REDD Programme. The workshops focused on strengthening national capacities on safeguards and multiple benefits; the participatory development of a national roadmap on safeguards, including the development of the national interpretation of the Cancun Safeguards and a policy framework.
  * May 2016 – a series of workshops and meetings were held focused on the development of REED+ PMA to feed into the development of the ENBIS. Work was carried out to produce the inputs needed to complete the draft document, including the identification of potential safeguards and risks; the interpretation of the Cancun safeguards; and a plan for the development of the SIS.
  * July 2016 – a series of workshops and working sessions were held focused on multiple benefits of REED+ in Paraguay. The workshops focused on strengthening national capacities, including national capacity building towards understanding and applying the Cancun safeguards in the development of REED+ projects; and on developing a draft document for the identification of project components. Between 2017-2018, the Board was involved in activities related to the design of the SIS.
* In addition, as part of the Bosques para el Clima/Soil Project, a Technical Committee was created. It was comprised of 12 public institutions linked to sustainable development and organized in working groups for the identification of REED+ project components. The Board was involved in activities related to the design of project components. Between 2017-2018, the Board was involved in activities related to the design of the SIS.

The REDD+ RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

### C.2 Use of proceeds and non-carbon benefits

#### C.2.1 Description of activities

A description is provided on how the process will be reinvested in activities consistent with the country’s NDC, National REDD+ strategy and/or low carbon development plans and policies. The description should also include information about the expected results and outputs related to the implementation of these activities plus activities, including non-carbon benefits.

Paraguay has approximately 18.5 million hectares of forest areas. Its deforestation rate has been one of the largest in the world with an average of 360,000 hectares per year. However, the country has begun to change through implementation of elements included in the National Strategy for Forests and Sustainable Growth (ENFSG) and the Environmental Social and Management Framework (SESAMF). The ENFSG aims at reducing deforestation rates, losses, barriers to change and legal/regulatory framework. This strategy is the result of an extensive participatory process involving all stakeholders including indigenous peoples, civil society organizations, academia, the private sector, and government agencies. It was developed in line with the National Climate Change Fund, which is expected to have a key role in financing actions towards Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs).

In view of the above, results-based payments will be invested in the implementation of the National Strategy for Forests and Sustainable Growth (ENFSG) and in the establishment and capitalization of the Climate Change Fund.

The strategic objectives of ENFSG are:
- To promote competitive agricultural and livestock production through the sustainable management of natural resources.
- To reduce the loss and degradation of forests by incorporating sustainable management practices.
- To improve forest governance, including strengthening the natural and cultural heritage of indigenous communities.
- To promote the use of payment at the municipal level.
- Improve climate change mitigation planning in the LULUCF sector.

The second strategic objective of the ENFSG is to strengthen institutional forest governance capacities including monitoring and enforcement. The use of RBPs have been divided into two main outputs, as follows:

Output 1 – Capacities, enabling environment and monitoring systems put in place for the implementation of the ENFSG


---
**Page 43**
---

The ENBICS provides the general framework for implementing concrete actions to address the direct and underlying drivers of deforestation and forest degradation. The set of activities grouped under Output 1 will support the implementation of REDD+ actions in accordance with the National Climate Change Fund (NCCF) strategy, by completing their zoning plans, strengthening the ongoing activities to reduce deforestation and support to sustainable land use, and supporting the development of a national carbon market.

The ENBICS will also contribute to the implementation of REDD+ actions by improving the institutional framework for implementing these actions. This includes strengthening the operational link between INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices, which are responsible for the implementation of REDD+ actions at the local level. The ENBICS will also support the development of a national carbon market.

The ENBICS will be used to significantly strengthen human resources and operational capacities both in INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices. This includes strengthening the institutional framework for implementing these actions, by improving the institutional link between INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices, which are responsible for the implementation of REDD+ actions at the local level.

The ENBICS will also contribute to the implementation of REDD+ actions by improving human capital investments. Particular attention will be given to reinforce inter-institutional coordination mechanisms between INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices, which are responsible for the implementation of these actions at the local level.

The ENBICS will be used to significantly strengthen human resources and operational capacities both in INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices. This includes strengthening the institutional framework for implementing these actions, by improving the institutional link between INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices, which are responsible for the implementation of REDD+ actions at the local level.

The ENBICS will also contribute to the implementation of REDD+ actions by improving human capital investments. Particular attention will be given to reinforce inter-institutional coordination mechanisms between INFORNA and MAFED/SEFENA regional offices, which are responsible for the implementation of these actions at the local level.

It is also key to significantly strengthen incentives for forest conservation. In this regards, Paraguay approved the establishment of a system of Payments for Environmental Services (PES; Law 30108). This innovative system will be implemented in the framework of the National Climate Change Fund (NCCF) and will be the source of payments. Rather, these take place between private actors with surpluses and deficits in the legal framework of PES. The objective is to provide incentives for forest conservation and make the mechanism fully operational. This will include (i) simplifying and digitalizing processes to substantially diminish transaction costs; and (ii) strengthening institutional capacities.

Strengthening the governance for territorial governance is an important action. The strengthening of territorial governance through MADE/SEFENA regional offices and mechanisms for planning and regulating land use (supported by INFORNA) will be a key element in this regard. This will also contribute to the implementation of PES, as it will help to reinforce the legal standing of public and private protected areas. Monitoring, supervision and enforcement will be strengthened.

The full respect of social and environmental safeguards including the mainstreaming of gender issues will be an action transversal to the whole set of activities financed by REDD+. As part of Output 1, NCCF will finance the implementation of a project that will support the development of a national grievance redress mechanism. The project will also contribute to the strengthening of territorial governance through MADE/SEFENA regional offices and mechanisms for planning and regulating land use (supported by INFORNA). This will be done in close collaboration with the National Environment Agency (ANAM) and the Ministry of Social Development (MIDES).

Paraguay over time less dependent on multilateral and other types of institutions for technical and administrative assistance in the management of external funds.

In view of the above, RBP's will be invested in defining the procedures for operation, responsibilities, accountability, transparency, integrity, and gender equality. RBP's will support the identification and definition of the bottleneck and constraints to the implementation of the NDC. The criteria for the selection of projects whose operation have been established, and sufficient safeguards are in place, the AE will proceed to disbursing RBP's to eligible projects.

There will be special requirements for the allocation of RBP's through the GCF. Contributions from RBP's will be kept in a separate sub-account and will be used to support activities in line with the GCF, the remaining contributions will be used as per the NDC. The contribution from RBP's will be used only if the projects funded by RBP's through the GCF will be consistent with the objectives of the GCF. RBP's may only be used for projects that are aligned to the GCF and will not be used in any other way. The use of RBP's will adhere to the ESFM, which constitutes an integral part of this Full Proposal package, and will be consistent with the NDC. The use of RBP's will also be subject to the approval of the AE and the verification of local and international experts verifying that the use of RBP's in compliance with the above stated conditions.

On the sustainability of the fund, the Law 578 establishes that contributions to the NCFC may come from (i) the annual resources contemplated in the National Budget; (ii) contributions, payment of rights and uses foreseen by law; (iii) other resources obtained through agreements or contracts with foreign countries; (iv) contributions made by other countries governments and international agencies; and (v) any other resources obtained through agreements or contracts with foreign countries. The Law 578 also establishes that the principles of transparency, participation and safeguard requirements will attract from-incoming from different sources. Transfers to the NCFC will be subject to the approval of the AE and the verification of local and international experts (in addition of environmental activities), in addition to contributions from the National Budget. The structuring of the NCFC also provides for a mechanism that allows the NCFC to receive resources from other funds, such as the investment vehicle, MADEs is already assessing options to ensure regular contributions to the NCFC from fees and fines.

The full implementation of the DNBCs should also result in the creation of "virtuous circles", where the decrease in deforestation rates opens the possibility of accessing new payments, which are then invested in forest conservation projects. This would create a virtuous circle that could lead to further reductions in payments. This would have a strong political effect in Paraguay by showing that the environmental sector can generate income and that it is possible to reduce emissions while generating economic benefits.

If the project is successful in establishing this dynamic, and therefore attract a regular flow of resources, the sustainability of the project will be ensured.

Annex B provides a description of the structure and operational modalities under discussion for the NCFC

Consistency with Paraguay's NDC:

The Government of Paraguay submitted its NDC in alignment with the National Development Plan for 2014-2030. The goal established by Paraguay in its NDC is to avoid emissions for 420 MCO2e during the period of implementation (from action plan 2014-2030) and from 2030 onwards to avoid emissions in the amount of 83 MCO2 eq. annually.

The NCFC established a target of 20% of emissions reductions relative to a projected BAU for 2030. The country sets a threshold of 15% of emissions reductions relative to a projected BAU for 2030, and an additional conditional target of 10% of emissions reductions relative to a BAU by 2030.

The Third National Communication on Climate Change (2017) determines the baseline according to the following parameters:

* Benchmark: National GHG Inventory with a base year 2000 presented in the Second National Communication
* Projected emissions: Projected National GHG Inventory using 2011 as the base year: 140 million tons of CO2 equivalent (under review)
* Projected National GHG Inventory with 2020 as the base year: 232 million tons of CO2 equivalent (under review)

# REDD-plus RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (PAGE 42 OF 83)

### Projected National GHG Inventory with 2030 as a base year: 416 million tons of CO2 equivalent (provisional figures)

The Second Biennial Update Report (2018) outlines the main lines of mitigation actions as part of the country's implementation of its NDC. The activities proposed for the use of the proceeds coming from this GCF REDD-plus project are:

- Reduce and avoid deforestation, through payment mechanisms for conservation and for reducing emissions.
- Encourage the implementation of agriculture with new technologies: reduction in the use of nitrogen fertilizers, reduction in the burning of land for new agricultural fields, introduction of technologies with climate-smart characteristics.

These actions are directly related to the AGRIC sector which is the greatest historical contributor of GHG emissions in Paraguay.

Paraguay is in the process of elaborating an NDC Implementation Plan, which integrates measures of the ENBCS as fundamental components for the process to reduce GHG emission by 2030. The ENBCS is a set of actions that are being developed and will be presented to the international community within the scope of the Paris Agreement.

The document proposes a series of outputs and expected outcomes, including financial mechanisms for the implementation of public policies aimed at reducing emissions as well as strengthening MRV mechanisms.

The results-based payments received by Paraguay from the GCF will contribute to the implementation of actions related to the ENBCS.

## C2.4: Budget estimate (for monitoring and reporting purposes)

Based on the ITAP evaluation and results of the scorecard, the volume of credits that qualifies for payments is 14,145,000 tCO2eq. At $5 USD/tCO2eq and an additional 2.5% for non-carbon elements, this translates into a payment amount of $73,869,000.

The budget presented in tables 13 and 14 has been developed through a joint effort by MINDEF, INFONA, STP, UN Environment and other stakeholders. The total of $50,000,000 USD reflects the ceiling of the Accommodation Fund for the period under consideration.

In regards to the volume of payments not included in the budget (below 224,851,125 USD), Paraguay may choose to present a subsequent proposal for these remaining funds at a later Board meeting.

UNEFP will be the Accredited Entity (AE) for this six-year project, as designated by Paraguay. The AE will be responsible for overseeing the implementation, financial management, evaluation, reporting and closure of the use of proceeds.

The national Executive Entity (NEE), will be Paraguay, managed by MACES. MACES will coordinate the national efforts to implement the National REDD+ Strategy (Paraguay’s National Plan on Climate Change) and will be accountable to the AE for project execution and for the effective and efficient use of resources allocated under this proposal in accordance with the GCF’s financial and reporting procedures.

At the request of the Govt. of Paraguay, and in line with the UN “Delivering as One” model, UNEP and specialized agencies such as FAO, UNDP, and UNOPS will provide support to MACES in their respective strengths. ### UNEP
As the AE, UNEP will be responsible for overseeing project implementation and evaluation in coordination with the Project Steering Committee (PSC) and the Project Management Unit (PMU). This includes inter alia, M&E activities; oversight of the PMU; ensuring that the PMU is executing its functions as per the Terms of Reference; overseeing the project to ensure consistency with GCF and UNEP's policies and procedures. The PMU will formally submit a draft project implementation plan for approval by UNEP and the Executive Board, including mid- and final evaluations; c) periodic progress reports and reviews of Project Implementation; and d) technical memos and other documents as required.

UNEP will establish clear roles and responsibilities for the parties involved in the delivery of the proposed activities, the timing and conditions of the fees, the determination of the fiduciary rules and the terms and conditions of the project. UNEP will also ensure that the PMU is executing its functions as per the Terms of Reference. Executing Entities as contracting parties to document the roles and responsibilities of each entity. UNEP will establish a Project Cooperation Agreement with UNDP to receive administrative support.

### PSC
The Secretaría Técnica de Planificación y Desarrollo Social (Secretariat for Planning and Economic Development) in the country NDA, its Executive Director, and representatives from the Participation Steering Committee is to oversee the submission of applications for CFP support on behalf of the Government of Paraguay. The PSC will ensure that all project activities are well coordinated and in line with national priorities as stated in the PND 2030.

### Project Steering Committee - PSC
The PSC will consist of representatives of the NNE (MADES), AE (UNEP), and NDA (STP); as well as representatives of, among others, INFONIA; an indigenous people's representative; and the Ministry of Agriculture. The PSC will be chaired by the Executive Director of the Secretaría Técnica de Planificación y Desarrollo Social. The PSC will provide project oversight and strategic direction, including a) supervision of project implementation; and b) review of project plans and project reports, including approval of any changes in project objectives, activities or timelines.

### Task Manager (PM)
The Task Manager (TM) will be responsible for project supervision to ensure consistency with GCF and UNEP policies and procedures, and participate in: a) annual PSC meetings; b) the facilitation of the Mid-Term and Final Evaluation; c) submission of Annual Performance Reports and relevant documentation; and d) technical reviews of project outputs.

### National Project Coordinator
The primary contact for all national project coordinator. She/he will: a) alert the PMC members of any relevant information and/or developments; b) Communicate the PSC's strategic decisions to the PMC; c) ensure that project activities are in line with the approved project plan; d) provide regular updates on progress, challenges, and lessons learned to the PSC and UNDP; e) be responsible for maintaining a strong relationship with UNDP to ensure financial management of the project is consistent with GCF policies and procedures; f) work closely with national and local authorities, as well as TAU, UNDP, and NGOs to effectively manage the local context.

### UNDP
UNDP will provide UNEP with administrative support during the duration of the project. The UNDP will sign an agreement with UNDP to transfer funds to be executed by the PMU and specialized UN agencies (FAO and IUCN) as per the Terms of Reference. This arrangement is deemed most appropriate. If the PSC considers it more effective, UNDP may also sign agreements for any of these organizations through its cooperation mechanisms (e.g., UN-DAF agreement, PCA, SFA or others).

### Project Management Unit - PMU
The PMU will be comprised of a National Project Coordinator (PC), a Gender Specialist, a Finance Specialist, a Biological Specialist, and an Administrative Officer. The PMU will coordinate activities between AE, ESD, and various partners to oversee the implementation of project activities.

### C2.6: Non-carbon benefits

Paraguay, with the support of the UN-REDD Programme and UN Environment, undertook a series of studies and spatial analyses in 2015 to identify areas where REDD+ actions could provide social and environmental benefits. These analyses also identified areas under threat of deforestation. This information provided guidance for the development of an Environmental Services Regime (ESR) in Paraguay, which includes potential for non-carbon specific REDD+ actions.

The studies include the following:

- **Mapping of multiple benefits of REDD+ in Paraguay:** The use of spatial information to support land-use planning (Mapa de los beneficios múltiples de REDD+ en Paraguay: utilización de la información espacial para apoyar la planificación del uso del terreno) in Paraguay. This report identifies areas with potential to provide multiple benefits from the implementation of REDD+ actions, including biodiversity conservation and protection of water resources.

- **Mapping of multiple benefits of REDD+ in Paraguay:** Additional analyses to support decision-making about REDD+ policies and measures (Mapa de los beneficios múltiples de REDD+ en Paraguay: análisis adicionales para apoyar la toma de decisiones sobre las políticas y medidas de REDD+) in Paraguay. These analyses were developed further to the integration of multiple benefits into ESR planning in Paraguay. An additional series of studies was conducted, which identified areas with potential for providing environmental services and benefits not previously considered. The benefits assessed were prioritized by national stakeholders as being of interest for REDD+ implementation in Paraguay.

The implementation of REDD+ policies has the potential to enhance the provision of non-carbon benefits. The provision of such benefits is explicitly considered in a number of policies and measures included in the Estrategia Nacional de Bosques para el Crecimiento Sostenible (ENBICS).

For example, the ENBICS includes measures aimed at enhancing the effectiveness of the Environmental Services Regime to enhance the effective functioning of the Environmental Services Regime. In this regard, the extension of forest areas included in the Environmental Services Regime has increased considerably from 2015 to 2018, as shown in the table below.

| Year | 2015 | 2016 | 2017 | 2018 | Total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Forest | 34,843 | 91,784 | 79,547 | 30,763 | 195,577 |

This measure has the potential to significantly contribute to the provision of key environmental services, such as the conservation of biodiversity, the control of water- or wind-induced erosion, and the control of soil saturation. The protection of these services would be particularly important considering the level of threat affecting the country's forest ecosystems.

# REDD-plus RFB FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (PAGE 6 OF 83)

Paraguay is a highly biodiverse country; it represents an ecotone of its central position in South America, which places Paraguay at the crossroads of ecosystems. Although Paraguay has lost much of its original forests and fauna species present in the country, it is estimated that there are 182 species of mammals, of which 39 are endangered; 40 species of birds, of which 15 are endangered; 76 species of reptiles, of which 18 are endangered; 85 species of amphibians, of which 15 are endangered; and 476 species of fish, of which 18 are endangered. Regarding flora, there is an estimated number of species between 8,000 and 13,000, of which 4,690 are recorded and 12% are endemic.

The country is also home to an important part of two of the most threatened ecoregions in the world due to habitat destruction: the Chaco-Brazilian Savanna and the Bosque Atlántico.

The dry tropical forest of the Gran Chaco Area, which is shared by Argentina, Bolivia, Brazil, and Paraguay, is one of the most important deforestation hotspots worldwide, largely due to the transformation of natural vegetation into agricultural land. The loss of native forests in the Gran Chaco region and other areas of the Gran Chaco would be insufficient to ensure the survival of a large number of endemic terrestrial vertebrates. Therefore, it is necessary that the area of protected forest in the Gran Chaco region increases; some form of protection would have a very positive impact. These measures include an increase in the area of land under protection and the creation of new protected areas such as the Bosque Atlántico National Protected Wildlife Area under Law 352 of Protected Wildlife Areas (SNASIP), as well as the Plan Nacional de Restauración Forestal y Plan de Parques de Paraguay, which can help to diminish the pressure on native forests. The Chaco Ecoregion is one of the world's biodiversity "hot spots." Only 13% of the original forests remain, mostly in a highly fragmented and degraded state. The loss of forests has been caused mainly by the conversion of forests for agriculture, especially soybean production, and in recent decades for the advancement of mechanized agriculture, mainly for soybean production.

The Bosque Atlántico of Paraguay, located in the eastern part of the country, is one of the 15 ecoregions of the Brazilian Atlantic Forest. It is considered a biodiversity "hot spot" because it contains many endemic species (considering endemic species of amphibians, mammals and birds, areas of interest for habitat diversity, fragile ecosystems, etc.).

## Areas in the Chaco region at risk of future deforestation. Areas with high importance for biodiversity conservation and at high risk of deforestation are shown.

**Source:** Woznick, J.J., Thorley, V. Kapos, A., Mis, S., Wronowski et al. (2015). Mapping multiple benefits of REDD+ in Paraguay: using spatial information for land-use planning. Cambridge, UK: UNEP-WCMC.

There are several measures in the ENBCS with significant potential to contribute to the conservation of the biodiversity associated with this ecoregion. Among them, the protection of forest remnants of the eastern region, which is currently under threat from deforestation and degradation, and the promotion of actions with forest cover in the eastern region, in place until 2030, the promotion of ecological connectivity actions in those forests.

Another important non-carbon benefit that could result from the implementation of measures considered in the ENBCS would be the mitigation of soil erosion. Forests in the eastern region play an important role in controlling water runoff and reducing sedimentation in streams and rivers. Deforestation and degradation of forested slopes can decrease the soil's capacity to store water, thus increasing surface runoff and sediment transport into water bodies downstream, increasing the risk of flooding downstream and leading to water scarcity during drier seasons in the year.

Soil particles carried by runoff contribute to increased presence of sediments in streams and rivers, which can result in reduced flow rates and increased turbidity. This is particularly relevant for the operation of the Itaipu dam, the world's largest hydroelectric power facility, jointly owned by the governments of Paraguay and Brazil, which generates 72.5% of Paraguay's electricity supply.

The Chaco region is much more vulnerable to wind-induced erosion. This type of erosion is a key factor of soil degradation in the region. The use of agricultural practices for livestock grazing purposes in the Chaco, based on practices developed for humid ecosystems, have caused soil degradation by

Source: PNC-ONU/GRID/GEAM/CONAFAR (2015). Mapa de los beneficios múltiples de REDD en Paraguay: análisis adicionales para orientar la toma de decisiones sobre políticas y medidas. Asunción, Paraguay: FACIPAR/FUNPARA

Forests in the Chaco region can also play an important role in controlling soil salinization and increases in groundwater levels, playing a key role in controlling water balance and salt distribution. The extreme low slopes of the Chaco make it very vulnerable to erosion by wind and water. In areas where forests have been cleared, this often results in flooding and salinization due to the rise groundwater levels. In these areas, the transformation from forested land to agricultural land has led to a decrease in soil fertility and increased runoff into rivers. This, in turn, causes the rise of groundwater and the transport of salts to the surface.12 This process can have serious consequences for agriculture and livestock productivity. It is therefore a key benefit that REDD+ implementation can provide in the Chaco.

In addition to the environmental benefits mentioned above, Paraguay also aims to enhance social benefits by promoting sustainable development through the conservation of forests. The rural population of Paraguay would indirectly relieve pressure on forests. In the same manner, many of the strategic measures for REDD+ implementation in the country have an explicit aim to reinforce "the role of local communities and indigenous peoples" in REDD.

While urban poverty has declined in recent years, more than one third of Paraguay's rural population still lives in poverty.13 Rural areas are characterized by low levels of education and poor access to health services. In Paraguay suffer from limited access to markets, weak infrastructure and few opportunities for agricultural production.

In areas with high forest cover and high poverty rates, there tends to be a relatively high dependence of communities on forests for their livelihoods, especially in adverse economic situations. REDD+, PADD +,

The percentage of the population below the poverty line decreased by 2.2% from 2015 to 2018. In rural areas, the REDD-plus RBP implementation has the potential to enhance livelihoods through improved access and measures in the ENBCS have the potential to enhance the livelihoods of rural populations, including indigenous communities.

Specific measures include:

### D. Investment Framework
The proposed REDD-plus results-based programme aligns with each of the four pillars of the Green Climate Fund for the activities that will be the end result for this full period of funding (2015–2017).

#### D.1 Impact potential
- Identify the potential of the programme to contribute to the achievement of the Fund's objectives and results areas.

The "Land-Use, Land-Use Change and Forestry" (LULUCF) sector has historically been the main source of greenhouse gas emissions in Paraguay. The largest part of deforestation occurred before agriculture. In 2015, the Second Biennial Update Report on Climate Change (BUR 2) quantified LULUCF emissions at a rate of 37%, behind those generated by the agricultural sector estimated at 53%.

**Figure 11: INGEI 2015 – Emissions by sector**

| Year | Agriculture | Forestry | Other |
|------|-------------|----------|-------|
| 2014-2015 | 68.97 | 20.53 | 10.5 |

Source: BURD/Paraguay

The calculation of the CO2 emissions reduction from gross deforestation in Paraguay for the period 2015–2017 was carried out using the methodology established by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) at the national level (NREL) for the period 2000-2015. This was assessed by a technical group of experts in accordance with the UNFCCC guidelines.

According to these calculations, emissions for the period 2000–2015 were on average 58.7 million tons of CO2 equivalent (tCO2e). For the period 2016-2017, emissions reached 45.3 million tons of CO2e. The avoided emission totaled 9.5 tCO2e.

**Figure 12: Emissions from avoided deforestation - Paraguay**

# REDD-plus RBP Funding Proposal

## NREF del Paraguay y Resultado de reducción de emisiones (REDD+ en toneladas CO2 eq)

| Year | Emissions Reduction |
| --- | --- |
| 2005-2016 | 8,000,000 |
| 2017 | 9,000,000 |

Source: Funesqui y S. Pizarro, REDD+ Technical Annex, 2018

Figure 12 shows the dynamics of avoided emissions in Paraguay for the reporting period 2015-2016-2017. However, it is from 2013-2014 that emissions from deforestation started to show a decline, which was accompanied by an increase in forest cover. The main reason for this change can be attributed to the causes on the cases of these decreases in deforestation. However, there are indications that a number of policies started to take effect during 2013-2014. These include the implementation of Law No. 7702/17, which established MADES. The UNREDD Programme, and later the FCPF, supported the national forest monitoring system (nFMS) in Paraguay. This system was designed to monitor deforestation and reforestation according to the legally required minimum forest area. The Zona Deforestación Law received successive extensions. In the period 2013-2014, MADES implemented a series of interventions aimed at reducing deforestation through intervention procedures related to deforestation, changes in land use, selective extraction of trees and others. These interventions were carried out with the support of the FCPF and UNREDD Programme, as well as office verifications. All these interventions took place with MADES as the enforcement authority. Law 5294 on the Forest Code was also implemented during this period. This law established a system for forest cover verification. In parallel, MADES advanced with the implementation of Law 3001/06 that established a system of permanent monitoring and control of deforestation and reforestation in accordance with the area of forest cover. More than half a million hectares received environmental certificates. NGAs also provided technical and financial support to MADES for the implementation of these interventions, which had a positive effect on deforestation rates.

In 2017 there was a significant spike in deforestation. As mentioned before, there is no causality analysis that can identify the contribution of different variables to changes in deforestation rates. One potential contributing factor could be the increase in cattle ranching and other agricultural activities. However, this has not had an effect on overall deforestation since the sum of both periods still equaled 2. During the period of the previous administration, Law No. 7702/17 was adopted in September 2017 to modify the article 42 of the Forest Code. This law established the reforestation of former reserves. This measure had a negative effect on land-use change in Paraguay.

However, the reaction of the public and civil society organizations was against this government's current policy. The NGOs were strongly opposed to the Law No. 7702/17, which increased deforestation, which had openly contradicted the environmental goals of both MADES and INFORNA. The robust reduction in deforestation observed during the period 2013-2014 was not maintained after the adoption of this law.

Finally, existing policies combined with the abolition of Decree 7702/17 appear to be having the desired effects. Preliminary data indicate that deforestation levels are again declining and reaching the 2015-2016 average

# D.2. Paradigm shift potential

Describe the degree to which the REDD-plus activity catalysed impact beyond a one-off programme investment.

The potential for results-based payments to generate a paradigm shift in Paraguay is substantial, largely due to its strategic location and the fact that it has already made significant progress on forest governance reform. The country is capable of generating significant economic resources in addition to those benefits achieved by protecting the environment and reducing deforestation. This is particularly true given the current political debate, where important actors see the need to view the environment as a cost rather than an investment.

The second reason is that the access-to-results-based payments has the potential to create a "before and after" scenario in terms of forest governance reform. The decrease in deforestation rates, the increase in deforestation rates, the possibility of accessing new payments both from multilateral and private sources, and the ability to generate additional economic resources for the country are all factors that can promote additional decreases in deforestation and thereby allow continued access to payments. We envision a scenario where the results-based payments will include the co-financing of investments by private sector, as a means to leverage the impacts of its funds.

The result-based payments obtained as a result of this proposal would be invested to finance the implementation of actions that are aimed at addressing the direct and indirect causes of forest loss: barriers to change and legal/legitimacy framework. As mentioned earlier in this document, the ENBCS was developed through extensive consultations with key stakeholders in which 300 representatives participated, 46% of whom were from the public sector, 25% from civil society organizations, 18% from private sector, and 11% from indigenous society. The ENBCS is based on the framework of the development processes proposed by the National Development Plan (PND), the National Forest Policy, the National Climate Change Strategy, the National Climate Change Policy, the National Climate Change Mitigation Plan, the National Forestry Policy, the Agrarian Strategic Plan, and the National Environmental Policy.

The ENBCS development was led by the Secretariat of the Environment (SEAM), MAFAS, INFORPA, FONAFIP and IND. This proposal has a national scope and is closely related to the goals and objectives of the institutional framework for forest governance reform in Paraguay. It also includes representatives from the business production sector, agricultural organizations and institutions representing indigenous peoples.

The actions listed in the ENBCS are already showing impact through a decreased deforestation rate. The results-based payments will be used to finance the implementation of these actions aimed at reducing deforestation. The priority actions for the use of the resources include, among others, the following:

- Strengthening of the Protected Areas System (both public and private areas).
- Auditing and updating of the National Forest Inventory Framework, including the application of penalties.
- Full compliance with Law 301 on Valorization Rebuttable on los Servicios Ambientales (Payments for Environmental Services).
- Strengthening of the Environmental and Forestry Information System (monitoring system).
- Support for implementing land-use planning.
- Implementation of a National Forest Management Framework.
- Strengthening of environmental and forestry management capacities.
- Engagement with local communities, including indigenous peoples and other local actors;
- Knowledge management and knowledge sharing; and
- Contributing to the Climate Change Fund for actions that will reduce carbon and non-carbon benefits.

These are in line with Paraguay's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDC). The targets proposed by Paraguay in 2015 is a 20% reduction in emissions compared to those year the 2008. These targets were developed taking into account that the net greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions for the year 2008 were slightly below 60 million tons of CO2 equivalent, and that the country's total GHG emissions have been maintained at around 13.5 million tons; hence it maintained over time, make significant contributions to the NDC targets.

Paraguay is in the process of developing its NDC Implementation Plan, which includes several measures from this ENBCS as key elements for the results of reducing GHG emissions by 2030.

# D. Sustainable development potential

The implementation of the EMBCS aims to mitigate climate change in the LULUCF sector, reduce deforestation and forest degradation, increase the sustainable use of forests through strengthening of natural resources management systems, and improve livelihoods for forest-dependent communities by promoting economic activities and through strengthening the role of local communities and indigenous peoples in the process.

## Contribution towards SDGs #13 and #15

The project will support local communities focused on fostering the adoption of improved production technologies, increasing the productivity of forests for timber products, and developing and incentivizing agroforestry, silvopastoral, and sustainable management of forests. Among the key environmental benefits are: (i) increased forest cover; (ii) reduced soil erosion; (iii) enhanced connectivity between natural protected areas; reduction of water-induced soil erosion that could contribute to losses in agricultural productivity; and (iv) improved livelihoods for local communities through economic activities and prevention of wind erosion. Forest conservation in the Chaco region is also critical for controlling both soil salinization and desertification, which are important for maintaining high agricultural and livestock productivity in adjacent agricultural lands.

## Contribution towards SDG 5

The EMBCS will contribute to gender parity by ensuring the participation of women in forest management activities through the implementation of the Gender Action Plan (GAP) and the Gender Strategy (ENGGC). The EMBCS aims to reduce gender gaps by ensuring the participation of women in forest management activities, reducing their vulnerability to poverty and food insecurity, and increasing their access to forests and dependency over forest resources between men and women; and by aiming to ensure equal access and social benefits related education, credit, and also land.

# E. Needs of the project

Increased global demand for commodities, and particularly for beef, soy, and soy derivatives (constituting circa 50% of the country's total merchandise exports), with the agriculture sector representing close to 20% of value added in the economy. The expansion of agricultural production has led to deforestation in areas that are being cleared for agricultural and livestock development. Land that remains forested and is not transformed

# REDD-plus RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (PAGE 1 OF 63)

For farming or agricultural purposes is often perceived as being site capital on which property taxes are paid, obtaining no benefits in return. Therefore, the opportunity costs of land use are high.

Given this context, significant resources are required to stabilize and further reduce the rates of deforestation and associated emissions in Paraguay. The institutional and regulatory framework is in need of strengthening to implement REDD-plus activities effectively. In addition, the capacity of public institutions and other relevant institutions have limited staff and capacities to be able to fully carry out their mandated functions and a very limited budget. The government's 2016-2018 financial plan allocated USD 35 million for the environment (total budget, with only 4% coming from the central government). Increases in budget allocation to MADES and INFONA, circa 0.17% of allocations within the government’s 2019 financial plan, are required to ensure compliance with the commitments made under the ENBCS and to support the implementation of the REDD-plus forest permits.

Climate finance can thus play a key role in supporting the country to address the institutional gaps at all levels of governance and the current regulatory path to effectively address deforestation through the implementation of the ENBCS.

In addition, with support from the result-based payments, the country will operationalize and overcome the current limitations on the capacity of MADES and INFONA and UN Environment Programme (UNEP) to operate. This will contribute to change individuals' perceptions through the provision of incentives that can make forests a source of livelihoods and income for rural communities, while also providing opportunities for revenue generation, thus ensuring that forests can be a source of sustainable wealth and livelihoods.

### D.S. Country ownership

The funding proposal is based on the country's own capacity-building plans (policies, climate strategies and institutions).

The funding proposal has been developed in close collaboration with MADES and INFONA and in close consultation with UN Environment Programme (UNEP) and World Bank Group (WBG). The project will be implemented by STP. As such, this project has full country ownership. The project is aligned with and will support Paraguay’s Plan Nacional de Desarrollo (PNAD 2030), the National Development Plan 2030, the NDC, the National Climate Change Policy, and ENBCS.

In 2011, the country started its REDD+ readiness phase to develop and strengthen national capacities for REDD+. The project will build on this experience by continuing with the same approach. It will follow a participatory process and a series of intersectoral dialogues. The Project provides a framework in which the development of the ENBCS can be implemented, as well as the implementation of the REDD+ readiness phase was possible. In 2016, Paraguay presented its ENBCS, following extensive stakeholder consultations with public and private actors, indigenous groups, farmer organizations, academia, finance sector and civil society.

The use of result-based payments will be a key element in supporting the implementation and dissemination of the ENBCS’s strategic measures. MADES and UN Environment will implement this project.

Reducing Emissions from Deforestation and Forest Degradation, the role of forests in climate change mitigation, and the need to address the drivers that are an mitigating climate change through result-based payments in developing countries. These result-based payments aim to support reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation (REDD+) by providing incentives for sustainable forest carbon stocks. # REDD-plus RBP FUNDING PROPOSAL

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND (PAGE 62 OF 83)

### The UN-REDD Programme carried out an analysis of Paraguay's main land-use changes linked to deforestation, including forest degradation, clear cuts and losses.

The analysis included synergies between REDD+ and Law 301/06 on Payments for Environmental Services (Vehículos de Servicios Ambientales - VSA) and the potential role of the ENBCS in being financed by result-based payments. The analysis covered Paraguay's entire territory.

At current forest carbon prices ($5 USD/tCO2eq), result-based payments can cover the opportunity costs of 41% of deforestation emissions. With the carbon price at $9 USD/t CO2eq, the coverage for opportunity costs rises to 81%.

The territorial distribution of this reduction is mostly concentrated in the Chaco region, where most important deforestation occurs and where there are large areas of degraded forest. The Chaco also contains extensive farms for livestock breeding and presents opportunity costs between 3.09 and 3.11 USD/t CO2eq. These results highlight the importance of the ENBCS for accommodating variations in farm costs, benefits, exchange rates and carbon content present in the farm.

The social and environmental benefits of forest conservation, as well as the social and environmental costs associated with deforestation, are also important factors that must be considered when designing policies which will make the implementation of a REDD+ mechanism even more appealing from a public perspective.

To date, economic analyses have revealed the importance of combining REDD+ result-based payments with a REDD+ policy framework. The most effective way to do this is through the implementation of the REDD+ implementation REDD+, which includes implementation of the Law, can be a fundamental tool in the effort to reduce deforestation and forest degradation. The use of Emission Reductions from Avoided Deforestation Certificates could result in competitive incentives for almost all of Paraguay's types of agricultural activities, with substantial benefits for small farmers. However, it is important to note that the economic analysis shows that these types of activities have a lower participation in current deforestation processes and that the eastern region presents a greater potential for deforestation.

It is worth noting that the economic analysis also mentions that the effectiveness of this type of combined policies depends on the government's capacity to monitor and audit current laws, particularly the Forestry Law and environmental regulations.

Without such capacities, the effectiveness of a REDD+ implementation decreases significantly. For this reason, investing in the implementation of the ENBCS has the potential of significantly increasing the effectiveness and impact of the REDD+ mechanism. The ENBCS will allow for the creation of an early warning system capable of real-time and early warning alerts, supervision of compliance with the Forestry Law, and territorial planning.

Finally, the mechanisms for allocating result-based payments and implementing measures have been defined by strict cost-efficiency criteria. A significant part of the resources will be channelled through the Climate Change Fund (FCC) to support the implementation of the ENBCS. This will allow for a better comparison of comparative advantages for the implementation of the different prioritized actions while at the same time minimizing direct carbon costs.

These are risks related to land management, that respond to shifting national circumstances and needs, such as in the application of Law 234/04 prohibiting forest transformation activities and the conversion of forest areas into agricultural lands. These laws have been applied since 2006 and have had a significant impact on deforestation throughout this region. There are several laws, regulations, and measures that were implemented to address these risks.

The review of the risk identification processes indicates that there were recurring concerns, most of them of social nature, related to the lack of access to land and timber resources, the lack of protection of indigenous peoples’ rights, the lack of tenure security for communities, the lack of information on forest management capacities, and loss of territorial planning. These risks are also linked with the increasing pressure from urbanization, the expansion of infrastructure projects, pressures to other areas, wildfires, and the advance of the agricultural frontier. The identification of these risks has been consistent throughout the period of the policies and measures (PAMS) that have constituted the ENBDA.

The existence of a solid and coherent framework for REDD+ in the country is reflected by the implementation of specific policies, actions, and measures for the implementation of REDD+ in the country built on collaboration between government institutions, civil society organizations, indigenous peoples’ organizations, as well as for applying the UN Environment Safeguards Framework, which is aligned with the safeguards standards of the World Bank. The framework also includes a series of specific policies, actions, and measures emphasizing the participation of indigenous peoples.

Platforms have been established to ensure access information, filing complaints, and promoting transparency. Over the past few years, the country has allocated resources to promote transparency and increase public participation in decision-making processes related to REDD+. These are institutional and technical processes that contribute to sustainable development. In that sense, information related to the protection of indigenous peoples’ rights is available through these platforms. The country also has mechanisms against corruption, promote access to information, and to control and sanction illegal acts was identified.

The implementation of laws, policies, actions, and measures for the implementation of REDD+ in the country are consistent with the requirements of applicable standards and safeguard policies and has not been related to practices that have caused significant environmental or social impacts.

The Environmental and Social Assessment carried out indicates that the country has strong legal instruments, which provide broad support to the individual and collective rights, through which they seek to minimize the negative effects on the environment and society. The country also has a strong institutional framework for development. Resources are invested in the implementation of complementary measures and actions. The results indicate that there is no significant risk associated with the environmental or social impacts related to the implementation of laws, policies, actions, and measures that considered criteria for risk mitigation and evaluation.

E.1.2 For the use of processes

Provide adequate and sufficient information describing how environmental and social risks and impacts will be identified, assessed, monitored, and managed during project activities. This includes the identification including the determination of the relevant environmental and social risk category of the proposed activities and the measures that are in place to mitigate these risks.

The Accredited Entity (AE), UNEP, has extensive experience in the management and implementation of environmental projects. Its participation in the UN-REDD Programme and the leadership it has demonstrated in recent years have provided a strong basis for its ability to provide technical assistance on risk identification, management, and monitoring, as well as actions to address and apply safeguards in an adequate pre-project phase.

The AE’s mandate and its Environmental, Social and Economic Sustainability Framework (ESSE) guide its actions towards the generation of sustainable alternatives that enable the conservation, management and sustainable use of forest resources while generating opportunities for local communities and key populations.

**Page 67**
