## d. Stakeholder consultation and participation

As mentioned above, the ENBSC has been conducted collectively with a gender approach and the promotion of participation of key stakeholders, including women’s groups and civil society. These key actors were identified and involved in the REDD+ work following the guidelines of the stakeholder identification and mapping process described in section 2.1 of the ESMP document. The discussion on strategies and measures it is carried out with representatives of the public sector, private sector, civil society, indigenous peoples’ organizations, and women’s groups. This was done through a series of workshops with a cross-cutting gender approach and continued engagement and participation of women and civil society organizations in the process of developing the ESMP, which was created and formed within the framework of the National Commission on Climate Change, with the aim of becoming a space for discussion and technical consultation on issues related to REDD+.

The ENBSC was conducted as part of the implementation of the Gender Strategy developed under the UN-REDD National Programme from 2011-2016, and continued under the FCPF “Forests for Sustainable Growth” programme. The Strategy was shared and validated through a process which involved 300 people in total.

This project for RBPA for Paraguay took as a starting point the diverse and broad consultations conducted during the development of the ENBSC: the documentation of this stakeholder engagement process is found in the following annexes (Annexes 1.2, 1.4). The Gender Strategy was also developed and adopted, and civil society organizations that work in women’s issues were engaged during the consultations conducted for the development of the ESMP. This was done through a series of workshops organized as a special session of the REDD+ Working Group; and with a meeting with members of the REDD+ Working Group and other representatives from civil society organizations, including the National Secretariat for Planning for Economic and Social Development (STP), the GCF Nationally Designated Authority, National Institute for Rural and Land Development (INERT), and National Forestry Institute (INFONA).

Additional information on the gender-sensitive approach to REDD+ and the role of RBPA in this regard is provided in the updated ESMP, regarding the stakeholder consultations on the use processes (see section 2.1 of the ESMP document). The specific concerns and issues raised are elaborated in the ESMP document, which was developed with the inputs received from civil society organizations and other actors.

## 4. Gender and Programme Management

The design of the project submitted to the GCF focuses on Capabilities, enabling environment and monitoring systems put in place for the implementation of the ENBC (Outcome 1), as well as Governance and operational procedures of Climate Change Fund established and financed through NDCs, the ENBC (Component 2) and the National Climate Change Fund (Component 3). The project, which is under Component 1 of the project, has benefited from comprehensive engagement with stakeholders, allowing perspectives to be considered in the design of actions. It is expected that the implementation will be carried out at the stage of stting, planning and designing actions. Staff involved in these processes will be capacitated on gender equality and women’s empowerment issues. The team responsible for identifying potential barriers to the engagement of women, and possible gender bias in the design of actions, which may lead to an unequal distribution of benefits and burdens, are recognized early on and managed accordingly.

Gender equality will also be integrated into the procedures for operation, responsibilities, accountability, structures and processes of the project. The project will be implemented through the National Climate Change Fund (Component 2) in several ways. The operation of the Fund as it concerns the use of result-based payments (RBP) to support the implementation of actions under Component 1 is expected to have a positive impact on access to resources by local stakeholders and indigenous communities. The criteria developed will also consider potential gender impacts, including those related to land tenure and forest management. The project could have inequitable negative impacts on gender equality and/or the situation of women and girls.

Proposed activities will not discriminate against women or reinforce discrimination or inequalities based on gender and will be implemented in coherence with the Paraguayan normative framework on the matter and the National Gender Policy (Política Nacional de Género) and the National Forest Policy (Política Nacional del Bosque Clínico).

Objectives relative to achieving gender equity through the participation of women in all areas of forest decision-making, as well equitable access to services, land and financial or investment credit, will be guiding principles for setting up, operating and evaluating the National Climate Change Fund. Proceeds from this proposal will be used to support making the Fund operational. As a first measure, processes will

To be invested to define the procedures for operation, responsibilities, accountability, structuring, and the criteria for allocation of resources of the Fund, in line with the principles of transparency, predictability, and gender equality. Once the rules of operation have been established, and sufficient safeguards are in place, the AE will proceed to disburse proceeds to capitalize the NCCF.

The National Climate Change Commission (NCCF), and a REDD+ Working Group which was formed by the Commission, were designed with a gender approach and gender equality as underlying principles. They will participate in the decision-making on the RBP funds that are allocated to the National Climate Change Fund, including the criteria for allocation of resources, and will be responsible for monitoring and reporting on the use of proceeds.

An administrative team will be available for the transparent management and implementation of the funds, which will include oversight of the governmental financing sources, and will also consider gender equity at all stages of project management and implementation.

A gender specialist will be hired for the Project Management Unit, which will help to ensure gender mainstreaming in the design and implementation of projects. The Project Management Unit will work with the gender specialist according to identified needs and to UN Environment and GCF guidance on gender.

Within the context of continuous management, risks will be continually evaluated and monitored, tools for continuous monitoring will be designed and implemented integrating gender equality considerations.

As detailed in the ESM, UN Environment will ensure that women's priorities, gender gaps and differences are integrated into project design. The UN Environment will also ensure that a participatory, gender-responsive approach will be taken throughout the project. Some criteria will apply to the design, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of projects:

- Accountability for gender-responsive approaches among project implementers, including UN Environment's personnel, country counterparts, partners and collaborators, which support women's participation in and the empowerment of women in discussions about and in the management of natural resources.
- Results-based management for gender equality by utilizing common-system indicators and measurement protocols.

## 5. Project implementation considerations and next steps

Vulnerabilities of women in Paraguay relate to poverty, inequality, and poor representation in decision-making structures at all levels. These vulnerabilities are complex, multi-dimensional, and often deep-rooted, and relate to many of the factors mentioned in this analysis, including access to land tenure security, education, health care, economic opportunities, political participation, and economic opportunities, among others. While progress has been made in recent years in Paraguay on issues related to accessing labour markets, health and education, there are still important gaps, as described in this analysis.

The main needs and priorities of women and men identified through stakeholder engagement activities undertaken in the preparation of the project include those related to access to natural resources, including for food, medicine, sustainable commercial and other livelihood-related activities; formalization of land tenure; and equal representation in decision-making structures. These are critical issues that matter both for men and women, but are more significant for women, namely access to natural resources, economic opportunities, political participation, and education.

The lack of secure land tenure is a major issue for many vulnerable groups, including a large number of vulnerable stakeholders, including indigenous peoples and small farmer’s communities. constitutes a recurring theme in the analysis of gender equality in Paraguay. The analysis also shows how a significant gap between men and women when reviewing rates of political participation, labour force participation, and access to education.

The project seeks to address these concerns by implementing gender sensitive and gender responsive actions and by mainstreaming gender in the design, implementation and monitoring phases of the project. Further information on the specific gender-sensitive actions is provided in Annex 1. The analysis, decision-making and implementation of actions has improved throughout the years. However, important gaps and needs remain. This is why the ENBCS was formulated and used in 2017.

This project will contribute to the achievement of the ENBCS goals and objectives and specific measures of the ENBCS, and its overarching approach which seeks to achieve gender equity through the participation of women in all areas of forest decision-making, as well as equitable access to services, land tenure or investments. The project will also contribute to the implementation of the National Action Plan to establish a land tenure formalization mechanism. The ENBCS actions, and other activities funded through the

# Gender Assessment and Action Plan

Document associated with the Proposal for REDD+ Results-Based Payments in Paraguay for the Period 2015-2017

## 6. Indicative Gender Action Plan for the use of Proceeds*

The Gender Assessment has identified important country-level gaps and needs, which have helped to inform the assessments and analyses that were included, as well as the formulation of the gender responsive action plan. The Action Plan will be implemented in a participatory manner, including integrating gender considerations in the processes, promoting parity in participation, adequate monitoring and evaluations, among others.

More specific indicators of local communities will also be analyzed further, at project inception, in line with the provisions and processes described in the ESMP. This process will also result in implementation and follow-up agreements to ensure effective implementation of the Action Plan by the Ministry of STP and INFONA, and AEI, as well as other relevant institutions.

An indicative timeline for implementing the Gender Action Plan is as follows:

- activity related to conducting assessments and analyses (year 1 and 2 of project implementation)
- activity related to integrating gender considerations (year 1 and 2, and follow-up hereafter)
- capacity building (at least once a year) in years 3–5
- monitoring activity (according to M&E processes, at least once a year; closer monitoring at the beginning of the project, and during the midterm evaluation)

* UN REDD+ document reports on the experience and good practices regarding this topic. The link can be found here.

**Note:** As well as, and in a necessary and significant contribution to implementing the pillars of the National Gender Strategy on Climate Change: (a) gender equality; (b) participatory decision-making; (c) financing; (d) education/communication; and citizen participation; (e) knowledge management and technology.

## Project Activities | Gender responsive actions | Indicators and targets | Responsibilities

### Output 2: Governance and operational procedures of Climate Change Fund established and financed channeled to NDCs, the ENBCS and low carbon development plans

#### Output statement:
Ensure women and men benefit equally from operation of the fund, and funding contributes to the reduction of gender gaps, particularly in the differentiated roles related to forests and dependence on forest resources between men and women.

#### Requirements for the establishment and operation of the Climate Change Fund
- Ensuring the fund operates transparently.
- Safeguarding against corruption.
- Facilitating participation modalities and decision-making mechanisms.

#### Integrating the gender equity objectives of ENBCS – ensuring equal participation of women in all stages of policy making, equitable access to services, land and finance – will be achieved by:
- Assessing the viability of gender equality considerations as eligibility criteria (e.g., representation in specific categories), and ongoing evaluation.
- Indicators: If analyses of how fund can act as a catalyst for addressing gender gaps.

#### Target: TBC at inception workshop
- Indicators: % of women’s groups and workshops associated with the design of the fund which took into account women’s constraints (location, age, ethnicity, community groups, etc.) and women’s interests and interest groups.
- Indicators: % of consultations and workshops associated with this activity which are designed to take into account for women's constraints and inclusion of women's interest groups.

(PMU)
Focal points within MADES and NOCC
